  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Sejong, leader of Korea (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Korea
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Turtle Ship, Replacing Caravel (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Hwach'a, Replacing Trebuchet (Requires technology: Physics)
        - Unique Building: Seowon, Replacing University (Requires technology: Education)
        - Unique Building: Chaebol, Replacing Stock Exchange (Requires technology: Electricity)
        - Civilization Ability: +1 Science from Specialists, increasing by +1 in Medieval, Industrial, and Atomic Eras. +20% Science during Golden Ages, and +50 Golden Age Points when a Great Person is born, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Sejong
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      
      ## Policies
      - Dictatorship of the Proletariat (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Dictatorship of the Proletariat: +7 Food, Science, Gold, and Culture per city.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - National Treasure (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Science from Artifacts.
        - * A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
        - * Gain 250 Gold when you construct World Wonders, scaling with Era.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Exchange Markets (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Trade Route.
        - * +1 Happiness for every active Trade Route.
        - * +15% Tourism modifier for Trade Routes.
        - * Resources from City-States count towards Global Monopolies.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 6
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 8
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 5

        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Persia-Byzantium peace treaty is catastrophic for anti-Persian coalition. Byzantium (17 delegates) no longer war with Persia. Korea now faces Persian 31,136 military nearly alone. Maintain current persona settings - Boldness (5) and WarBias (6) already elevated to reflect full-scale war reality. DiplomaticBalance remains high (9) to preserve The Huns alliance (critical for Aluminum). Byzantium peace with Persia reduces coalition reliability but doesn't require Korea to abandon diplomatic outreach - Byzantium still shares Order ideology and Defensive Pact with Korea.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Great Leap Forward (Policy)
        - Rationale: Select Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Order Tenet) instead of Communism. Persian Golden Age (8 turns remaining) means Persia likely preparing major offensive. Great Leap Forward provides: (1) FREE Technology - immediately advances research progress toward Rocketry/Stealth, accelerating access to Jet Fighters/Guided Missiles. (2) Spies gain +100% Science - Korean spies in Persian cities (Bactra, Nicaea, Adrianople) can steal critical technologies. (3) +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens - significant science boost given Korean population (106 across 4 cities). Communism's 25% gold investment reduction is less valuable than immediate technology boost during existential Persian threat.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Morocco
        - Public: -5
        - Private: -5
        - Rationale: Maintain Morocco relationship at (-5, -5). No change needed despite Byzantium's new war declaration against Morocco. The slight negative stance signals mild disapproval of Morocco's past trade route aggression against Byzantium (300g plundered), but doesn't escalate further. Maintains economic cooperation while registering displeasure. Byzantium's war with Morocco is their strategic decision; Korea maintains independent stance focused on Sweden.
        - UpdatedTurn: 260
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Polynesia relationship to neutral baseline (was -5,-5). While Polynesia competes for city-state influence and votes against Korea's interests in World Congress, the Swedish war is nearing conclusion and peace treaty expires in 7 turns. Maintaining overly negative stance without active conflict limits diplomatic flexibility. Polynesia has strong relationships with Persia (DoF/Defensive Pact) and Sweden, so preserving neutral relations prevents unnecessary complications. Polynesia's 12 delegates make them valuable in World Congress; neutral stance may allow future cooperation or at least prevent active obstruction. Reset to 0,0 for pragmatic post-war diplomacy.
        - UpdatedTurn: 308
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Poland relationship to neutral (0,0) from (-10,-10). Sweden's war declaration on Poland (turn 379, event 26) creates strategic opportunity. Poland (Freedom) now fights both Persia (Autocracy) and Sweden (Autocracy) - our common enemies. With Polish naval forces engaging Korea but Poland also resisting Persian expansion, softer stance may enable diplomatic cooperation to redirect Polish military focus toward Persia. The limited Korean-Polish conflict (mostly northern naval skirmishes) doesn't warrant continued hostility when Sweden (threat 2259 vs Persia 16480) weakens anti-Persian coalition. Neutral posture preserves flexibility for future alliance against Persian threat while signaling willingness to reduce tensions.
        - UpdatedTurn: 379
        
        ### Sweden
        - Public: -40
        - Private: -40
        - Rationale: Increase Swedish hostility to (-40,-40) from (-20,-20). Sweden declared war on Poland this turn (event 26), attacking a Freedom civilization that opposes Persia. This reveals Sweden as aggressive expansionist rather than focused ally. Sweden's 2259 military attacking Poland weakens the anti-Persian coalition (Persia 16,480 military remains primary threat). With peace treaty expiring soon and Sweden already destructive (sanctioned, denounced Korea, spies caught), harsher stance reflects their true threat level. Sweden attacking Poland while Persia's Golden Age continues shows misplaced priorities - they're the wrong enemy, warranting maximum diplomatic hostility to justify stronger military response and deny cooperation.
        - UpdatedTurn: 379
        
        ### The Huns
        - Public: 120
        - Private: 120
        - Rationale: Maintain maximum relationship (120,120) with The Huns. Polynesia's proposal to sanction The Huns in World Congress threatens Korea's primary Aluminum supplier (3+4 Aluminum deals). Sanction would severely restrict Aluminum access critical for Jet Fighters and spaceship components needed for Science Victory. Maximum relationship ensures The Huns vote against their own sanction and provides diplomatic leverage. SC voting in 3 turns - need strong Huns support to block Polynesian sanction that would cripple Korean military-industrial complex.
        - UpdatedTurn: 392
        
        ### Byzantium
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Reduce Byzantium relationship from (70,70) to (30,30). Byzantium signed peace treaty with Persia (event 4-11) despite defensive obligations and shared Order ideology alliance. While Byzantium still maintains Defensive Pact and DoF with Korea, their peace with Persia during Korea's critical war (Persian Golden Age, 31,136 military vs Korean 12,907) is a major betrayal of coalition objectives. Reduction from 70 to 30 signals significant displeasure without severing alliance ties - Byzantium's 17 delegates still needed for World Congress (Order vote, blocking Huns sanction). Maintains positive relationship for cooperation opportunities while registering diplomatic cost of Persia peace.
        - UpdatedTurn: 395
        
        ### Persia
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -60
        - Rationale: Increase Persian hostility to (-60,-60) from (-40,-40). Persia killed Korean Paratrooper (event 24) despite signing peace with Byzantium this turn - shows Persia's war focus remains squarely on Korea. Persian naval bombardment of Jeonju continues (events 17, 19, 21, 63, 64, 67, 71, 72), city at 48% health. Persian Atomic Bombs rebased to Nicaea/Adrianople (events 91-94) near Korean territory - nuclear threat escalating. Persian ground forces advancing through Korean lands (Nicaea, Adrianople zones). Maximum hostility (-60) reflects existential threat and justifies all defensive measures, nuclear deterrence (UseNuke: 80, Nuke: 95 flavors), and total war mobilization.
        - UpdatedTurn: 395
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale:  naval to 92 (from 95) -10 Persian ships around Jeonju overwhelm 反 naval 反 Wireless Air countermeasure defense. Diplomacy 提升 sector 65 (from 60) - critical World Congress vote turn: vote FOR World Ideology Order, AGAINST Sanction The Huns (protects Aluminum supply). Maintain high Defense/CityDefense (100) - Jeonju at -91% health requires emergency defense. UseNuke 85/Nuke 95 preserved - Persian naval saturation may justify tactical nuclear response if Jeonju capture imminent. Spaceship/Science/Production remain maxed - despite war, continue race toward 科技胜利 but defense first.
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 43
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 10 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Byzantine Sphere of Influence: Argos:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Sweden:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Monaco:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Ragusa:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Polynesian Sphere of Influence: Huari:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Onondaga:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Polynesian Sphere of Influence: Utica:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Byzantine Sphere of Influence: Aztlan:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Eastern Orthodoxy:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1

        You, Sejong (leader of Korea, Player 4), are making strategic decisions after turn 397.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Sejong, leader of Korea (Opponent 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Korea
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Turtle Ship, Replacing Caravel (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Hwach'a, Replacing Trebuchet (Requires technology: Physics)
        - Unique Building: Seowon, Replacing University (Requires technology: Education)
        - Unique Building: Chaebol, Replacing Stock Exchange (Requires technology: Electricity)
        - Civilization Ability: +1 Science from Specialists, increasing by +1 in Medieval, Industrial, and Atomic Eras. +20% Science during Golden Ages, and +50 Golden Age Points when a Great Person is born, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Sejong
      - OpponentID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      
      ## Policies
      - Dictatorship of the Proletariat (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Dictatorship of the Proletariat: +7 Food, Science, Gold, and Culture per city.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - National Treasure (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Science from Artifacts.
        - * A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
        - * Gain 250 Gold when you construct World Wonders, scaling with Era.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Exchange Markets (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Trade Route.
        - * +1 Happiness for every active Trade Route.
        - * +15% Tourism modifier for Trade Routes.
        - * Resources from City-States count towards Global Monopolies.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #1:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Locking in Great Leap Forward as our next policy branch choice. The free technology and massive science boost to spies and cities are crucial to maintaining our scientific supremacy and securing a spaceship victory while under heavy military pressure.
        - Policy: Great Leap Forward
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: -100
        - Rationale: Persia has captured our major city of Jeonju and is on the verge of a cultural victory. They are our existential threat. We must set our relationship to the absolute minimum hostile level to ensure our staff and allies treat them as a mortal enemy and coordinate all efforts to dismantle their empire.
        - Private: -100
        - TargetID: 1
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-persona
      - args:
        - WarBias: 9
        - Rationale: In response to the loss of Jeonju and the imminent threat of Persia's victory, we must dramatically increase our victory competitiveness, boldness, and war bias to mobilize every resource, take calculated tactical risks, and fight back with maximum aggression to reclaim our lost territories.
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 8
    - #4:
      - toolName: keep-status-quo
      - rationale: Our current flavors (Defense 100, CityDefense 100, Mobilization 90, Production 95, Science 100) are perfectly optimized for our dual goals of surviving Persia's onslaught, securing our borders, and racing towards a spaceship victory. We will maintain this macro-level focus.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Tech Tree**
            
            My focus has shifted to a detailed examination of the available technologies, particularly in light of my current research target. I'm re-evaluating the immediate research path and its implications for my overall Grand Strategy.
            
            **Evaluating Tech Options**
            
            My attention is now on the technology tree. I've noted Refrigeration as a research option and am reviewing our policy selections, particularly the availability of "Great Leap Forward." I'm also re-assessing our immediate strategic posture following the loss of Jeonju to Persia.
            
            **Assessing Military Losses**
            
            I'm reviewing the significant military and city losses, particularly Jeonju now being occupied by Persia. I'm analyzing the remaining forces and their dispositions around Seoul and Busan to understand our current strategic vulnerability.
            
            **Assessing Strength Disparities**
            
            I'm scrutinizing the troop strengths and unit compositions, noting our significant deficit compared to Persia. I've identified our naval presence and Byzantium's surprisingly low military power, despite our defensive pact.
            
            **Clarifying Diplomatic Alliances**
            
            I'm re-examining our alliances and wars. It seems Byzantium, despite our defensive pact, is at peace with Persia. I'm also noting Persia's pact with Polynesia and the Huns' war with them. This significantly changes my strategic outlook.
            
            **Stopping Cultural Victory**
            
            I am realizing Persia is perilously close to a Cultural Victory, having achieved influence over all other major civilizations. I'm now focused on preventing this by leveraging our superior science output to potentially win via spaceship parts, while simultaneously fending off Persia's aggressive military advance and evaluating our next policy choice.
            
            **Choosing Next Policy**
            
            I am currently weighing the benefits of "Great Leap Forward" against "Patriotic War." The free technology from "Great Leap Forward" is tempting for our science victory, but "Patriotic War" offers crucial defensive strength for our current situation against Persia. I am leaning towards the science boost for long-term victory.
            
            **Selecting Next Tech**
            
            My current focus is on selecting the next technology. Stealth is nearing completion, and `Refrigeration` is the only listed option thereafter, promising trade route and offshore platform benefits. I'm verifying if any other technologies are available before committing.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomacy Settings**
            
            I'm now fine-tuning diplomatic standings. I've set our relationship with Persia to maximum hostility and am assessing potential future conflicts, particularly with Poland whose peace treaty expires soon. I'm also checking Byzantium's readiness to join the war.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Threats**
            
            I am re-evaluating our diplomatic standings, particularly regarding Sweden, who is friends with Persia and hostile towards us. I'm also noting our strong alliance with the Huns and their ongoing war with Polynesia, while considering the upcoming expiration of our peace treaty with Poland.
            
            **Setting Research and Policy**
            
            I'm finalizing my next steps. Research will now focus on Refrigeration, with Stealth nearly complete. Simultaneously, I'm confirming "Great Leap Forward" as our next policy, prioritizing scientific advancement while assessing defensive needs.
            
            **Confirming Grand Strategy**
            
            I am confirming the Great Leap Forward policy and setting our relationship with Persia to maximum hostility due to their occupation of Jeonju and nearing cultural victory. I will maintain current strategic flavors, focusing on a spaceship victory and strong defense, after reviewing recent combat logs detailing the heavy bombardment of Jeonju.
            
            **Moving Naval Forces**
            
            I am directing our naval assets from the west coast towards the east to bolster the defense of Seoul and Busan. Our western fleet, comprising destroyers and fleet destroyers, needs to redeploy rapidly to counter Persia's significant naval presence and prevent further territorial losses.
            
            **Adjusting Strategic Persona**
            
            I am now considering modifying our strategic persona, specifically by increasing our Victory Competitiveness to the maximum. This reflects the urgent need to counter Persia's imminent Cultural Victory and pivot towards securing our own win condition, likely via spaceship, while also re-evaluating coastal defense needs.
            
            **Updating Strategic Persona**
            
            I'm now adjusting our strategic persona, specifically by increasing Victory Competitiveness to 10 and Boldness to 7. I'm also setting Forgiveness to 1, as we won't forgive Persia. I'll then implement these changes using the `set-persona` tool to reflect our aggressive stance and focus on winning.
